## OpenCode Review (grok-review)

I'll review the five plans against the current source and the approved concept, without changing any files.
## 06.49-01

**Summary:** The before-measurement wave is correctly first and uses a real list page. `GuardianPublisherListView` at `tests/test1/app/views.py:716` already has `cv_list_actions = ["detail", "update", "delete"]`, and `Table` adds the actions column at `src/crud_views/lib/table/table.py:28`. Default `inaccessible_actions` is `hide` (`src/crud_views/lib/settings.py:57`), so denied Edit/Delete links are absent. The 120/80 navbar cells, nine ancestor JSON slices, and five child cases match the current tests.

**Strengths:**
- Production stays unchanged until exact before pins exist. The four constructors are still at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:153`, `:210` and `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:67`, `:70`.
- List admission does not need a model permission: `GuardianQuerysetMixin.has_permission` returns true for an active user, and `get_objects_for_user` excludes the ungranted row.
- Navbar, ancestor, and child verify commands match the current parametrization, including serial child runs and `CV_ANCESTOR_COSTS_DIR`.

**Concerns:**
- **MEDIUM:** `.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-01-PLAN.md:67` requires list-filter grant subqueries to be distinct from checker grant SELECTs, but the cited `sql_categories` at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1034` cannot do that. A Publisher list query is `FROM "app_publisher"` and is labeled `parent_rows`. Checker loads are `FROM "auth_permission"` joined to `guardian_userobjectpermission` / `guardian_groupobjectpermission`, so they fall into the same `permissions` bucket as vocabulary lookups. Copying that helper produces a baseline plan 04 must treat as immutable.
- **LOW:** “each Details link present” is false for the zero-row case. Assert link counts equal `row_count`, not a nonempty Details set.

**Suggestions:** Count checker loads by the guardian grant join and `object_pk` predicate, and count `get_objects_for_user` by the `app_publisher` outer query plus its grant subquery. Record manage `auth_user_groups` queries separately; they are fixed overhead, not row-action grants.

**Risk Assessment:** Low implementation risk, medium evidence risk. A mislabeled before receipt cannot be reconstructed later.

## 06.49-02

**Summary:** The helper boundary matches the concept. Only four constructors exist, hooks already receive the user, and keeping `_request_checker` as a delegate preserves the patch at `tests/test1/test_access_trace_guardian.py:247`. A local `from guardian.core import ObjectPermissionChecker` still sees the constructor patch at line 57. The three-call test at line 514 asserts `has_perm` calls, not constructions, so reuse does not break it.

**Strengths:**
- Decisions stay in the existing callers. Inactive actors return before a checker at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:197`. Anonymous users are not inactive at line 32, so a direct `cv_has_access` can reach the checker at line 210.
- F-01 exemption is exact and applied at both checks in `tests/test1/test_link_access.py:469` and `:476`.
- The link-helper state test is correctly separated from ordinary row actions, which still evaluate state before permission at `src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:941`.

**Concerns:**
- **HIGH:** `.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-02-PLAN.md:109` cannot pass as written. `tests/test1/conftest.py:128` sets `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME=None`. `ObjectPermissionChecker(AnonymousUser())` calls `get_identity`, which calls `get_anonymous_user()` (installed `guardian/utils.py:129`). That looks up `username=None` and raises `DoesNotExist` before any grant check. The plan never sets the name or creates that database user. The existing working pattern is `tests/test1/test_delete_databases.py:42`: monkeypatch `guardian_settings.ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME` to a created user’s username, grant that database user, then pass a separate `AnonymousUser` instance.
- **LOW:** “no view stores a checker” has no automated assertion. After F-06 is rewritten, a leftover `view._cv_guardian_ancestor_checker` would not fail a test.

**Suggestions:** In the anonymous test, set the Guardian name for that test only, grant the database user, and assert the checker attribute is on the supplied `AnonymousUser`, not on the resolved database user. Add one assertion that neither own nor ancestor call sets a view attribute.

**Risk Assessment:** The helper itself is small and safe. The required anonymous regression fails until the setting override is added.

## 06.49-03

**Summary:** D-37 is applied only to the workflow permission cases, and the revoke really survives the refusal. `remove_perm` runs in the test override before `super().cv_form_valid_process` (`tests/test1/test_workflow_review.py:233`), while `transaction.atomic` starts inside that super call (`src/crud_views_workflow/lib/views.py:131`). A later Client request therefore sees the revoked grant. The four F-06 cases are `MODES` × `DEPTHS` at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:49` and `:54`.

**Strengths:**
- Scope and action cases, scoped reload, and no-write assertions stay. Modal 422 already comes from `cv_form_invalid` at `src/crud_views/lib/views/mixins.py:99`.
- F-05 refetch points are right: `rendered_data(..., actor)` at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:615` reuses the Python user, while `client.get` does not. The cancel view is already bound, so assigning the fresh user to `view.request.user` is required.
- Next-request identity is separated from the denied request, so a 403 cannot hide the checker comparison.

**Concerns:**
- **LOW:** The follow-up must not hit the stock `/campaign/<pk>/workflow/` route. That route is the production view, not the local `GuardianWorkflow`. Build urlpatterns inside the test, as `tests/test1/test_link_access.py:699` does. The cited `test_navbar.py` does not contain that pattern.
- **LOW:** F-06 should assert the view instance lacks `_cv_guardian_ancestor_checker`. The current class/ViewSet loop at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:552` does not inspect the instance.

**Suggestions:** Assert original `loads`/`checked` lengths before the follow-up Client request, as the plan already allows. Use `force_login`; the fixture password is unused by middleware.

**Risk Assessment:** Low. The lifetime rules are pinned without a freshness bypass, provided the follow-up uses a new request user.

## 06.49-04

**Summary:** After pins are measured, not projected. The named F-03 equalities that will break are inside the five selected tests, notably `len(rendered) == len(baseline)` at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1307` and `:1361`. Ancestor tests pin guard order, not checker totals, so leaving `test_ancestor_costs.py` unchanged when counts do not move is correct.

**Strengths:**
- The same fixture and cache protocol as plan 01 makes the before/after comparison valid.
- Navbar measurement reuses `view.request.user` after the page request (`tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:124`). The plan correctly says to keep that checker and describe priming instead of clearing it.
- Historical `BASELINE_SHA` at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_costs.py:51` is explicitly preserved.

**Concerns:**
- **MEDIUM:** The after attribution inherits plan 01’s classifier gap. If checker joins and vocabulary lookups share the `permissions` bucket, a lower total cannot prove “same row shares grants, next row loads again, list filter does not fill the checker.”
- **LOW:** `test_query_card_occurrences_remain_independent` at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1238` says every decision uses a fresh checker. It does not pin a count, so it should pass, but the comment will be false. The combined file run will not force that comment to be updated.

**Suggestions:** Pin per-row checker-load counts from the captured SQL: one user/group load per visible row, not per action, and an unchanged list-filter subquery. Update the stale occurrence comment in the same file edit.

**Risk Assessment:** Medium until the SQL signatures are explicit. The authorization assertions around the pins are otherwise retained.

## 06.49-05

**Summary:** Documentation targets match the stale passages: `docs/reference/navbar.md:346`, `docs/reference/context_buttons.md:245`, the ancestor module text at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:21`, and the extra false claim at `docs/reference/guardian.md:467`. Final commands use environments that exist. No Breaking entry is planned, which matches the concept unless execution finds one.

**Strengths:**
- Published numbers are required to come from plan 04 receipts, with owner/deep priming kept separate.
- The final gate reruns the full Django 6 suite, ancestor/trace suites, frozen matrix, examples, and Ruff, and treats missing interpreters as limits.
- Independent paths stay out of the helper: `get_objects_for_user`, backend `has_perm`, delete projection, and bulk review/execute.

**Concerns:**
- **LOW:** No automated check ties a published navbar or context-button integer to a row in `06.49-COST-BASELINE.md`. A hand-copied number can pass `mkdocs build --strict`.
- **LOW:** The anonymous documentation must say the lookup depends on `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME`. This project’s default is `None`, so a universal “one anonymous query” claim would be wrong.

**Suggestions:** In the validation file, quote the published table cells beside the receipt rows and runtime versions. State that `refresh_from_db` does not drop `_cv_guardian_checker`.

**Risk Assessment:** Low once plans 01 and 02 are corrected. The closure gate is broad enough to catch an unlisted same-user assertion without adding a bypass.

## Cross-plan

The wave order is right: measure, replace acquisition, adapt lifetimes, reconcile counts, then document. D-01 through D-09 and F-01 through F-08 are allocated, and no public cache API or `prefetch_perms` path is introduced.

Two gaps should be fixed before execution. Plan 02 must configure `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME` for the required anonymous hook test, or that test raises under `tests/test1/conftest.py:128`. Plan 01 must define checker-load SQL separately from `sql_categories`, or the D-38 before/after receipt cannot support the sharing claim plan 04 and the navbar table depend on.

# Cross-AI Plan Review — Phase 06.49: Reuse Guardian object grants across navigation checks (#385)

Reviewed against repository source at `HEAD` (`dc327b07`), `superpowers/specs/2026-11-23-link-access-check-design.md` (`4b65990f`, §§7, 12.1, 12.3, 12.7, 13), and the phase `.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/` artifacts. No files were modified.

## 06.49-01

### Summary
Establishes the pre-change measurement baseline required by D-06 and concept D-38 (`tests/test1/test_guardian_checker_costs.py` and `.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-COST-BASELINE.md`) using real `Client` responses against `GuardianPublisherListView` (`row_count=[0, 1, 3]`) and records starting-tree receipts for the 120-cell isolated navbar matrix, ancestor cost slices, and five F-03 child-link query tests before any production edit.

### Strengths
- **Strict pre-mutation sequencing (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-01-PLAN.md:67-74`):** Enforces recording the starting git SHA, unchanged four `ObjectPermissionChecker` construction sites (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:153, 210` and `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:67, 70`), and real SQL receipts before Wave 2 touches production code.
- **Reuses existing tracked ViewSet and table view (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-01-PLAN.md:59, 67`):** Uses `GuardianPublisherListView` and `cv_guardian_publisher` (`tests/test1/app/views.py:709-719`), which already declares `cv_list_actions = ["detail", "update", "delete"]`, avoiding global ViewSet registry side effects.
- **Authorization assertions paired with cost pins (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-01-PLAN.md:67, 117`):** Verifies that granted `detail` links render, ungranted `update`/`delete` links are absent, and ungranted `Publisher` rows are filtered out by `GuardianQuerysetMixin.get_queryset` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:337-367`).

### Concerns
- **[LOW] `sql_categories` buckets Django model-permission queries and Guardian checker grant queries into the same `"permissions"` key (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-01-PLAN.md:65, 68`):**
  - *Mechanism:* Task `01-01` references `sql_categories` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1034-1056`) and asks to distinguish list-filter row queries from user/group checker grant SELECTs. While `sql_categories` already classifies outer `SELECT ... FROM "app_publisher"` queries (including `get_objects_for_user` grant subqueries) as `"parent_rows"` (`line 1041`), its `'from "auth_permission"' in sql` branch (`line 1045`) combines Django model-permission queries (triggered by `GuardianPublisherListView`'s default `"create"` context action calling `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired.cv_has_access` -> `user.has_perm("app.add_publisher")` at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:374`) with `ObjectPermissionChecker` user/group grant queries (`guardian/core.py:124, 144`). Additionally, default registration appends `"manage"` to `cv_context_actions` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1178-1180`), which issues a `FROM "auth_group"` query (`"other"`).
- **[LOW] Task `01-02`'s child-link `pytest -k` filter omits `test_query_card_occurrences_remain_independent` (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-01-PLAN.md:99`):**
  - *Mechanism:* `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1228-1257` runs `order="repeated"` with `objects = [parents[0], parents[0]]` (`line 1235`) on a single `actor` and records `report_measurement(record_property, layer="occurrences", order=order, total=len(capture), categories=categories)` (`line 1256`). Because it only asserts `parent_rows == 0` and `child_or_relation_rows == 0`, it did not fail in the section-12.7 probe, but its `order="repeated"` measurement property drops from `permissions: 5, total: 6` (`06.48-COST-BASELINE.md:531`) to `permissions: 3, total: 4` once the second occurrence of `parents[0]` reuses `actor._cv_guardian_checker`.

### Suggestions
- In `tests/test1/test_guardian_checker_costs.py`, classify outer `FROM "auth_permission"` queries that join `"guardian_userobjectpermission"` or `"guardian_groupobjectpermission"` separately from model-permission queries (or explicitly warm/control `cv_context_actions` as in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1180`), so the D-38 row-action checker grant SELECTs (`6 * row_count` before vs `2 * row_count` after for 3 row actions) are directly visible.
- In Task `01-02` (and `04-02`), record all `test_query_` properties in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py` (with `-o junit_family=legacy --junitxml=...` as in `06.48-COST-BASELINE.md:522`) in addition to the serial 5-test F-03 run so `test_query_card_occurrences_remain_independent[repeated]` is also captured before and after.

### Risk Assessment
- **Risk Level:** Low. Production code is untouched in Wave 1; tightening SQL classification and capturing the full `test_query_` property set prevents ambiguity in Wave 4.

## 06.49-02

### Summary
Adds `_get_guardian_checker(user)` in `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/permissions.py` storing `_cv_guardian_checker` on the user instance, replaces the two constructor sites in `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:153, 210` and the two branches in `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:67, 70` (keeping `_request_checker(view, user)` as a thin view-free delegate), updates `ancestors.py` module docs (`lines 21-23`), exempts `_cv_guardian_checker` in `tests/test1/test_link_access.py:469, 476` (F-01), and adds focused helper/target/anonymous tests in `tests/test1/test_guardian_checker.py`.

### Strengths
- **Preserves existing test and trace seams without unnecessary churn (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-02-PLAN.md:66, 80-81`):** Keeping `from guardian.core import ObjectPermissionChecker` inside `_get_guardian_checker` and retaining `_request_checker(view, user)` as a thin delegate to `_get_guardian_checker(user)` preserves all three monkeypatch seams in `tests/test1/test_access_trace_guardian.py:57, 247, 514`. In particular, `test_repeated_object_gates_execute_again_but_identical_reports_deduplicate` (`lines 508-530`) passes `user` three times to `get_object()`; reusing the mocked `checker` instance on `user._cv_guardian_checker` still invokes `checker.has_perm` three times with `side_effect = [True, True, False]`.
- **Exact F-01 exemption (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-02-PLAN.md:110`):** Exempting only `"_cv_guardian_checker"` at both set comprehensions in `tests/test1/test_link_access.py:469, 476` preserves the stage-1 anti-decision-caching check and its dynamic `gate`/`own`/`state` loop (`lines 479-499`).
- **Maintains strict guard boundaries (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:141-212`, `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:75-105`):** Inactive-actor checks, `_check_nested_auth` database validation, missing-permission checks, and `accept_global_perms` model fallback all execute before `_get_guardian_checker(user)` is called.

### Concerns
- **[MEDIUM] `test_anonymous_direct_hook_reuses_checker` in Task `02-02` will raise `User.DoesNotExist` unless `guardian.conf.settings.ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME` and its database user are set up in the test (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-02-PLAN.md:109`):**
  - *Mechanism:* Task `02-02` directs: `"Add test_anonymous_direct_hook_reuses_checker using actual AnonymousUser and guardian.utils.get_anonymous_user with real object grants."` However, `tests/test1/conftest.py:128` sets `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME=None` globally. When `ObjectPermissionChecker(AnonymousUser())` (`guardian/core.py:62`) calls `get_identity(AnonymousUser())` -> `get_anonymous_user()` (`guardian/utils.py:48-50, 61-63`), Guardian queries `User.objects.get(username=guardian_settings.ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME)` (`username=None`), which raises `django.contrib.auth.models.User.DoesNotExist` because Guardian's anonymous user creation is disabled when `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME=None`.

### Suggestions
- In Task `02-02` (`test_anonymous_direct_hook_reuses_checker`), follow the pattern established in `tests/test1/test_delete_databases.py:38-42, 681`: create a backing `User` instance in the database (e.g., `anon_db = User.objects.create_user(username="guardian_anon")`) and `monkeypatch.setattr(guardian_settings, "ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME", anon_db.username)` (plus clear any cached anonymous user if applicable) before calling `get_anonymous_user()`, granting the object permission to `anon_db`, and calling `cv_has_access(AnonymousUser(), obj)`.

### Risk Assessment
- **Risk Level:** Low-Medium. The production helper change is minimal (~15 lines across 3 files) and well-bounded; addressing the `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME=None` test configuration avoids an immediate `DoesNotExist` error during Task `02-02`.

Adds grant-lifetime and request-isolation regressions to `tests/test1/test_guardian_checker.py`, adapts only the `outcome == "permission"` branch of `test_guardian_workflow_late_refusal_retains_scoped_authorization` in `tests/test1/test_workflow_review.py:251-272` (D-04/D-37/F-02) with a follow-up `Client` request verifying 403 on the next request, and updates the F-05/F-06 tests in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:611-615`, `tests/test1/test_cancel_permissions.py:635-637`, and `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:546-555`.

- **Faithful implementation of D-37 / F-02 (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-03-PLAN.md:80-81`):** Preserves `initial-denial`, `action`, and `scope` refusal branches in `tests/test1/test_workflow_review.py:251-272`, retains the `loads` and `checked` reload assertions (`lines 261-270`), and adds a real `Client` follow-up request to prove that the revoked permission denies the next request with 403 without adding any workflow-specific cache bypass in `src/crud_views_workflow/lib/views.py:96-104`.
- **Accurate F-05 handling for bound view requests (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-03-PLAN.md:102`):** Explicitly notes that in `tests/test1/test_cancel_permissions.py:631-637`, `view` was already bound with the pre-grant `user` at line 631, so re-fetching `user` after `assign_perm("view_book", user, book)` at line 635 also requires updating `view.request.user = user` (or rebinding `view`).

- **[LOW] Follow-up `Client` request in `test_workflow_review.py` should verify that `_check_object_perm` executed on the second request (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-03-PLAN.md:80`):**
  - *Mechanism:* In `tests/test1/test_workflow_review.py:221-224`, `GuardianWorkflow` is a function-local subclass of `ScopedWorkflow(CampaignWorkflowView)` (`tests/test1/app/views.py:635`) that records `checked.append(obj)` inside `_check_object_perm`. When mounting `GuardianWorkflow.as_view()` in a temporary URLconf for the follow-up `Client` request, asserting only `response.status_code == 403` would not distinguish a genuine `_check_object_perm` denial on the fresh `request.user` from an accidental 403 (e.g., if `client.force_login` was omitted or failed and `cv_guardian_anonymous_behavior` were 403, or if a view-level gate rejected before `get_object`).
- **[LOW] `SimpleLazyObject` identity comparison and module docstring in `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py` (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-03-PLAN.md:103`):**
  - *Mechanism:* In `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:546-550`, `first.request.user` and `second.request.user` are `SimpleLazyObject` wrappers created per request by `AuthenticationMiddleware`. Even after forcing evaluation, `first.request.user is not second.request.user` only compares the wrapper objects unless `_wrapped` is inspected. Additionally, the module docstring at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:14-15` still says `"Resolved objects and the Guardian checker live on the view instance of one request"`.

### Suggestions
- In `tests/test1/test_workflow_review.py`, after running the original assertions (`len(loads) == 2`, `len(checked) == 2`) for `outcome == "permission"`, perform the follow-up `Client` request (using `override_settings(ROOT_URLCONF=...)` and `clear_url_caches()` in a `finally` block) and assert `len(loads) == 3` and `len(checked) == 3` in addition to `status_code == 403`.
- In `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:550`, assert `getattr(first.request.user, "_wrapped", first.request.user) is not getattr(second.request.user, "_wrapped", second.request.user)`, `first.request.user._cv_guardian_checker is not second.request.user._cv_guardian_checker`, and `"_cv_guardian_ancestor_checker" not in vars(first)`, and update the module docstring at `lines 14-15`.

### Risk Assessment
- **Risk Level:** Low. Only test files are modified in Wave 3, and the targeted `pytest` command in Task `03-02` (`test_parity_child_collection`) cleanly avoids running the unupdated F-03 query assertions in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py` before Wave 4.

## 06.49-04

### Summary
Re-measures and updates query count pins in `tests/test1/test_guardian_checker_costs.py`, `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py` (120 cells), `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py` (F-03 cases), and `tests/test1/test_ancestor_costs.py`, appending the after tables and SQL attribution to `.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-COST-BASELINE.md`.

### Strengths
- **Correctly distinguishes `page == "owner"` vs `page == "deep"` priming in `test_navbar_isolated_costs` (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-04-PLAN.md:73`):** In `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:119`, `page == "owner"` dispatches `Host` (`tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:45-49`), which is a plain `DetailView` that does *not* run a Guardian check before `CaptureQueriesContext`, so navbar rendering loads `owner` grants once (dropping from 4 to 2 Guardian permission queries). By contrast, `page == "deep"` dispatches `Deep` (`tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:77-86`), whose `_cv_ancestor_guard()` checks the `ns.owner` `GuardianViewSet` ancestor during `client.get(url)`, priming `current_actor._cv_guardian_checker` before `CaptureQueriesContext` (dropping navbar Guardian permission queries from 4 to 0). Task `04-01` explicitly accounts for this mechanism.
- **Preserves `test_ancestor_costs.py` historical baseline and invariants (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-04-PLAN.md:95`):** In `tests/test1/test_ancestor_costs.py:257-266`, the supplementary `list-with-create-button` slice already passed `rendering_view=self` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:333`), so it already reused the view-local checker and its counts do not change.

### Concerns
- **[MEDIUM] Unnoted mid-test grant mutation at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1353` in `test_query_unaffected_core_and_ordinary_actions` will fail `assert links.detail.cv_has_access(actor, parent) is True` at line 1358 (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-04-PLAN.md:94`):**
  - *Mechanism:* Task `04-02` instructs for the five F-03 cases: `"Keep actual actor lifetimes intact: first check may load grants and later rendering can reuse them. Replace obsolete equality between differently primed observations with separate exact measured first-load/subsequent-render values; do not refetch an actor to manufacture equality."` However, in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1320-1371`:
    1. The `world` fixture (`line 649`) grants `actor = actors[0]` only `"change"` (`change_publisher`) on `parent = parents[0]`, **not** `"view"`.
    2. In the first half of `test_query_unaffected_core_and_ordinary_actions` (`lines 1335-1350`, `layer="mixed_core_guardian"`), `rendered_data(links, "context", actor, parent)` checks the `CHANGE_READ` parent gate (`line 76`) on `parent`. This populates `actor._cv_guardian_checker._obj_perms_cache[(ctype.id, str(parent.pk))] = ["change_publisher"]`.
    3. At `line 1353` (the start of the second sub-scenario, `# Ordinary Guardian row action cost is unchanged relative to the original access call`), the test grants `"view"` to the **same** `actor` on the **same** `parent`: `user_guardian_object_perm(actor, links.parent, "view", parent)`.
    4. At `line 1358`, `assert links.detail.cv_has_access(actor, parent) is True` checks `"view_publisher"` on `parent` using `actor._cv_guardian_checker`. Because `parent`'s grants were already cached on `actor._cv_guardian_checker` as `["change_publisher"]` at `line 1338` (before `"view"` was granted at `line 1353`), `links.detail.cv_has_access(actor, parent)` returns **`False`**, failing the test before the query-count comparison at `line 1361` is even reached.
- **[LOW] Stale comment in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1238`:**
  - *Mechanism:* `test_query_card_occurrences_remain_independent` at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1238` still has the comment `# Both positive and negative decisions use a fresh checker each time.`, while Task `04-01` only mentions updating fresh-checker comments in `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:44-45`.

### Suggestions
- In Task `04-02` for `test_query_unaffected_core_and_ordinary_actions` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1320-1371`), either:
  1. Move `user_guardian_object_perm(actor, links.parent, "view", parent)` from `line 1353` to `line 1326` (before the first `CaptureQueriesContext` loads `parent`'s grants into `actor._cv_guardian_checker`), in which case `baseline` (`line 1358`) and `rendered` (`line 1360`) both reuse the already-loaded grants on `actor` (`0` queries each), **or**
  2. Re-fetch `actor` immediately after the grant mutation at `line 1353` (matching F-05, since `line 1353` mutates grants in the database between the two sub-scenarios), in which case `baseline` (`line 1358`) measures the first load (`2` permission queries) and `rendered` (`line 1360`) measures the subsequent reuse on the same `actor` (`0` queries). State clearly in `04-02` which of these two options is intended so the executor does not hit an unexpected `AssertionError: assert False is True` at `line 1358`.
- Update the stale comment at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1238` alongside `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:44-45`.

### Risk Assessment
- **Risk Level:** Medium. Without accounting for the mid-test `user_guardian_object_perm(actor, links.parent, "view", parent)` grant mutation at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1353`, `test_query_unaffected_core_and_ordinary_actions` will fail on `links.detail.cv_has_access(actor, parent) is True` due to D-37 same-user grant caching from the earlier loop in the same test.

## 06.49-05

### Summary
Updates `docs/reference/navbar.md`, `docs/reference/context_buttons.md`, and `docs/reference/guardian.md` with the user-owned checker lifetime, D-37 workflow-refusal reload behavior, D-38 `prefetch_perms` exclusion, and reconciled cost tables; runs the doc suites, `mkdocs build --strict`, full Django 6 pytest suite, focused Django 4.2/5.2 compatibility checks, Bootstrap 5 example tests, and Ruff checks; and records final evidence in `.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-VALIDATION.md` and `06.49-COST-BASELINE.md`.

### Strengths
- **Comprehensive documentation and contract verification (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-05-PLAN.md:75-90`):** Covers all F-07 passages plus the additional `docs/reference/guardian.md:465-468` passage identified during research, and verifies against `test_link_access_docs.py`, `test_navbar_docs.py`, `test_ancestor_docs.py`, `test_snippet_docs.py`, and `mkdocs build --strict`.
- **Honest multi-environment verification gate (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-05-PLAN.md:99-121`):** Runs the full Django 6 suite, standalone ancestor/trace suites, focused Django 4.2/5.2 suites, `examples/bootstrap5` suite, and Ruff, while explicitly distinguishing `PYTHONPATH=src:.` checkout runs from installed-distribution certification.

### Concerns
- **[LOW] Clarity of the `8/6/4/2` table in `docs/reference/context_buttons.md:233-246` and cross-references in `docs/reference/nested.md:584`, `docs/reference/card-list-view.md:326`, and `docs/migrating-1.0.md:156` (`.planning/phases/06.49-reuse-guardian-object-grants-across-navigation-checks-385/06.49-05-PLAN.md:75-76`):**
  - *Mechanism:* The `8/6/4/2` strict Guardian parent column in `docs/reference/context_buttons.md:233-238` (and cited in `docs/reference/nested.md:582` and `docs/reference/card-list-view.md:323`) comes from `test_query_plain_child_link_cost` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1079`), which evaluates a single plain child link under a Guardian parent on a fresh user whose Guardian checker has not yet loaded that parent's grants. Those cold-checker numbers remain `8/6/4/2`, whereas on a host page that already loaded the parent's grants onto `request.user` (or on a second child link for the same parent object in the same request), the 2 Guardian grant SELECTs are reused (0 additional grant queries). Meanwhile, `docs/reference/nested.md:584`, `docs/reference/card-list-view.md:326`, and `docs/migrating-1.0.md:156` also say `"repeated Guardian links can still query permissions"`, which is now only true across distinct parent objects (e.g., distinct cards on a card list page without `prefetch_perms`), not repeated links for the same parent object in one request.

### Suggestions
- In Task `05-01`, when updating `docs/reference/context_buttons.md:233-246` and `docs/reference/guardian.md:465-468`, state explicitly that:
  1. The `8/6/4/2` figures in `context_buttons.md` measure a check where the parent's Guardian object grants have not yet been loaded onto `request.user` in that request; once `request.user`'s checker has loaded that parent's grants (e.g., via the page's own object/ancestor gate or an earlier link on the same object), subsequent checks on that parent reuse the loaded grants with `0` additional Guardian grant queries.
  2. On card/list pages with multiple distinct parent objects, each distinct object still loads its own grants because `prefetch_perms` is not enabled (D-38).

### Risk Assessment
- **Risk Level:** Low. Documentation and final verification gates are well-structured and preserve all existing doc-test assertions in `tests/test1/test_link_access_docs.py`, `tests/test1/test_navbar_docs.py`, and `tests/test1/test_ancestor_docs.py`.

## Cross-Plan & Overall Analysis

### Completeness & Alignment with Approved Concept
- **Concept compliance:** All five plans strictly respect `superpowers/specs/2026-11-23-link-access-check-design.md` (§§7, 12.1, 12.3, 12.7, 13) and the developer-maintainability rule in `CLAUDE.md`. No cross-request cache, decision cache, `prefetch_perms`, public helper API, or stage-3 link-check expansion is introduced.
- **Wave dependencies:** The 5-wave sequential structure (`01` before-measurements -> `02` helper & core regressions -> `03` lifetime & F-02/F-05/F-06 adaptations -> `04` after-measurements & count-pin updates -> `05` docs & full regression gate) is clean, has no same-wave file conflicts, and ensures `06.49-COST-BASELINE.md` captures genuine pre-change figures before any production edit.

### Summary of Actionable Adjustments Before/During Execution
1. **`06.49-02` (Task `02-02`, Medium):** In `test_anonymous_direct_hook_reuses_checker`, configure a backing anonymous `User` row and `monkeypatch.setattr(guardian_settings, "ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME", ...)` (as in `tests/test1/test_delete_databases.py:38-42`), because `tests/test1/conftest.py:128` sets `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME=None`.
2. **`06.49-04` (Task `04-02`, Medium):** In `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1353` (`test_query_unaffected_core_and_ordinary_actions`), handle the mid-test `user_guardian_object_perm(actor, links.parent, "view", parent)` grant mutation (by granting `"view"` before `line 1335` or re-fetching `actor` at `line 1353`) so `links.detail.cv_has_access(actor, parent)` at `line 1358` does not fail against the stale `["change_publisher"]` grant cache loaded at `line 1338`.
3. **`06.49-01` / `06.49-03` / `06.49-05` (Low):** Isolate Guardian checker grant SELECTs from model-permission SELECTs in `test_guardian_checker_costs.py`, include `test_query_card_occurrences_remain_independent` (and its stale comment at `line 1238`) in the child-link cost capture, assert `len(checked) == 3` on the follow-up `Client` request in `test_workflow_review.py`, and unwrap `SimpleLazyObject` (`_wrapped`) in `test_ancestor_boundaries.py:550`.

## Consensus Summary

### Agreed Strengths

All three reviewers confirm the five sequential waves capture the real starting-tree baseline before changing the four checker construction sites; the user-owned helper remains bounded; target permission/state decisions and existing trace seams stay in their callers; and D-37’s workflow refusal behavior is intentional. Request-shaped lifetime tests and the final preservation suites are appropriate. No reviewer demonstrated a need for a public cache API, invalidation path, prefetching, or broader production refactoring.

### Agreed Concerns

- **H-01 — HIGH: ordinary-action cost subscenario mutates a grant after that actor’s grants were loaded.** Fable rates this HIGH; Gemini independently describes the same failure as MEDIUM, and coordinator source reading confirms it. `world` gives the actor only change permission (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:645`); the loop uses that actor’s parent gate (`:1335`); the same actor then receives view permission (`:1353`) before `cv_has_access` is expected to allow (`:1358`). D-37 makes the saved grant set stale. Plan `06.49-04-PLAN.md:94` schedules numerical reconciliation and forbids refetching merely to manufacture equality, but does not allocate the additional grant-mutation fixture adaptation. **Needed:** explicitly choose preparation before the first checker load or a fresh actor at the genuine second-subscenario lifetime boundary, then keep first/subsequent observations on the same actor within that subscenario, preserve allow/deny outcomes, and measure actual counts. No production freshness exception is needed.
- **H-02 — HIGH: anonymous direct-hook regression lacks its required Guardian test configuration.** Grok rates this HIGH; Fable and Gemini independently identify the same defect as MEDIUM. `tests/test1/conftest.py:128` sets `ANONYMOUS_USER_NAME=None`; installed Guardian copies that value at `.nox/tests-3-12-django-6-0/lib/python3.12/site-packages/guardian/conf/settings.py:7`, and the anonymous lookup at `guardian/utils.py:62` cannot find the backing user. Plan `06.49-02-PLAN.md:109` asks for a real anonymous grant test without setting up that identity. **Needed:** create a backing database user and monkeypatch Guardian’s own settings constant for this test, as `tests/test1/test_delete_databases.py:42` already does; then use a separate supplied `AnonymousUser` and assert checker ownership and allow/deny behavior. This is fixture configuration, not a new product feature or global setting change.
- **A-02 — MEDIUM: duplicated published cost/lifetime wording is outside the documentation task.** Fable and Gemini identify `docs/reference/card-list-view.md:321` and `docs/reference/nested.md:580`; Gemini also identifies `docs/migrating-1.0.md:156`. These repeat the cost figures or repeated-link statement while plan `06.49-05-PLAN.md:75` updates the canonical context-button explanation. **Needed:** add bounded edits or an explicit source-backed disposition for those three existing passages. Preserve stage-1 migration guidance, qualify first-load versus already-loaded grants and distinct-object loads, and retain historical figures only with their actual measurement conditions. This does not request a new Breaking entry or migration section.
- **A-04 — LOW: stale lifetime prose remains in tests being edited.** All three flag the “fresh checker each time” comment at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1238`; Fable also notes the ordinary-action equality wording at `:1351`; Gemini identifies `tests/test1/test_ancestor_boundaries.py:14`–15, which still assigns the checker to a view instance. **Needed:** name these small comment/docstring updates in tasks 03-02/04-02 so the tests explain user ownership and first/subsequent loads correctly.
- **Covered consensus concern — SQL attribution:** all three warn that existing `sql_categories` conflates permission SELECTs. Plan `06.49-01-PLAN.md:68` already requires outer list-filter queries with grant subqueries to be distinct from user/group checker grant SELECTs, and `06.49-04-PLAN.md:72` requires attribution to actual checker/list-filter/ContentType SQL. The helper appears in `read_first` as a pattern; the plan never mandates copying it unchanged. Those executable requirements cannot be satisfied by relabeling the existing broad `permissions` bucket as checker grants. A bounded local classifier in the new test is an implementation of the existing task, not an unresolved new task. This concern is retained here but excluded from the actionable count.

### Divergent Views

- **A-01 — MEDIUM: changed exact cost pins lack cached Django 4.2/5.2 verification.** Fable identifies a concrete coverage gap; the others approve the broad closure gate without addressing this detail. The commands in `06.49-05-PLAN.md:113` and `:115` omit the new list-cost module and the navbar, child-link, and ancestor cost modules. Whole-response SQL totals can differ across supported Django series; the full Django 6 run does not verify those new pins on the other cached series. **Needed:** add those four cost modules to the two existing cached-version selections, with the stated six workers and measurement conditions, or explicitly dispose of this compatibility risk in the plan. No new environment installation or full Python matrix is requested.
- **A-03 — MEDIUM: no non-Breaking changelog entry covers the observable lifetime change.** Fable points to `.planning/PROJECT.md:177`, which requires an entry for public-surface changes. Plan `06.49-05-PLAN.md:67` excludes a Breaking entry, but its file/task list also omits any ordinary entry even though retained-user hook answers and the approved workflow refusal response change. **Needed:** schedule a concise `### Changed` entry in `CHANGELOG.md` explaining grant reuse, retained-user lifetime, and the D-37 response, or record an explicit justified disposition in the plan. The approved “no Breaking expected” decision remains intact.
- Fable suggests removing the `_request_checker` delegate and replacing the workflow Client follow-up with RequestFactory. Grok and Gemini endorse keeping the existing seam and real-request coverage. The plans already choose both deliberately (`06.49-02-PLAN.md:66,81`; `06.49-03-PLAN.md:66,80`); no redesign is needed.

### Other findings and counting dispositions

| Raw feedback | Current disposition and existing PLAN coverage |
| --- | --- |
| Store duplicate before constants in the test module | The immutable before receipt is already an explicit persisted artifact (01:68; 04:64,72). No loss-of-evidence case was shown; duplicate constants are optional, not an unresolved defect. |
| Details link must be scoped to actions; zero-row nonempty concern | 01:67 requires exact visible rows and links in the row action region. “Each” row at zero rows is vacuous; it does not require a nonempty Details set. Existing task covers both. |
| Enumerate every expected intermediate full-suite failure | Cost adaptations are allocated to plan 04, with broad suite only in plan 05. Plan 02’s verification selects checker/trace/inactive/matrix tests rather than running the full known-red suite. An extra intermediate full-suite inventory is not required by these tasks. |
| Assert no retired checker on views/classes | 02:87 already requires no view storing a checker; 03:103,107 requires instance ownership and class/ViewSet prohibitions. An automated assertion is a possible way to satisfy that acceptance, not missing execution scope. |
| Local workflow route, authenticated follow-up, and observation isolation | 03:66,80 explicitly require the same local class under a temporary URLconf, normal authentication, and original scoped/count assertions before or separately from follow-up observations. A stock-route substitute is already forbidden by the task. |
| Require a third `checked` entry to rule out hypothetical incidental 403 | The same-class authenticated follow-up and separate normal-request identity tests are already scheduled (03:74–85). A precise spy assertion may support that test but is not a separate uncovered behavior; no actual competing 403 path in the specified permission case was demonstrated. |
| Compare underlying lazy users, not merely wrapper objects | 03:103 already requires distinct actual wrapped users and checkers, with lazy users forced before inspection. `_wrapped` is one implementation of this existing acceptance, not new scope. |
| Add every child `test_query_` receipt, including repeated-card before measurements | 01:87–92 explicitly bounds the required before child receipts to the five F-03 cases; 04:95,107 runs the full current combined costs for preservation. Extra unpinned measurement expansion is optional under D-09; D-38’s required repeated-action measurement is the new real list test. No extra benchmark was invented or run. |
| Automate documentation numbers against the receipt | 05:78,81,99–103 and final verification already require numbers trace to actual receipts and runtime/cache conditions. A new test duplicating document numbers was not required; source review remains a valid verification for this prose change. |
| Universal anonymous-query or default anonymous support claim | 02:109 already forbids a universal anonymous SQL count; no documentation task promises direct anonymous access when the backing user is disabled. H-02 makes the supported configured test setup explicit. |
| Mixed runtime labels, refresh_from_db reset claim | 05:75–78 explicitly requires recorded versions and cache axes and forbids describing refresh_from_db as a reset. Already incorporated. |

### Current count and consensus gate

Three actual, source-cited, prompt-fed reviewers ran; none has a leading discount marker, and none is diff-only. The two HIGHs assert correctness of planned test execution (judgment class), and both additionally have materially overlapping independent reviewer findings. Both therefore count under the consensus gate. No HIGH was suppressed as single-reviewer/unconfirmed. Severity disagreements do not erase a corroborated HIGH. The four distinct actionable non-HIGH concerns above require executable PLAN amendments or explicit PLAN dispositions. Covered requirements, optional alternative designs, duplicates, and informational source/fact-drift limitations are excluded.

`CYCLE_SUMMARY: current_high=2 current_actionable=4`

No product execution was claimed by this review. The writer-owned conflict slot remains `None.` and can be changed only by plan-phase.
